Workout Date: 2021-08-04

	[Beau Baggett]: Thank you, Nick. I’ll get the email over to support later today. I am really excited and motivated to continue working with you. I’m measuring BF% on a bioimpedance scale. I realize it may not be precise, but I only really use it to track trends. I always use it first thing in the morning on days I measure to ensure a consistent level of (de)hydration. There’s no golden number I’m shooting for in terms of BF%, I just know from experience that my body type doesn’t wear fat well, so I just want to keep it in check. For mountain hunting fitness, most years I’ll know at least 3 months in advance if I’m going West to hunt, but there could be times when I only know 30-45 days ahead of time due to how leftover elk tags are sold. These trips would normally be during the first few weeks of September. To prep for a mountain hunt, I ramp up the cardio training with running and rowing as well as doing 2 days a week of heavy rucking for 30-90 minutes. My thought for this goal is to always maintain a sufficient level of conditioning to allow me to get in “mountain shape” within 30-45 days. The path we’re on now with conditioning work is great, I just wanted to get this potential scenario out on paper. Enjoy the BLOC Party!
	[Beau Baggett]: Thanks for the clarification on the BF% and the mountain shape. It is great working with someone like you who is in tune with their body and what you want to get out of it. I am pumped to be working with you!

Instructions
We are going to use RPE as a way of communicating how much weight to use for accessories. RPE stands for Relative Perceived Exertion. It is a scale from 1-10 with 10 being an all out maximum effort with nothing left in the tank. An RPE of 9 would leave 1 rep in reserve and an RPE of 8 would leave 2 reps in reserve.

Deadlift
Perform these "Ninja-style" with a slow eccentric portion from the knees down to the floor. Don't make any sound as you set the weight on the floor. Hold your position tight in the bottom for 1-2 seconds and then lift for the subsequent reps. Do not lose any tightness at the bottom. Take your breath at the top of each rep when you are standing.

	[Beau Baggett]: Hey Beau,Here's a link to schedul an intro call: https://calendly.com/cameroncox/monthly-nutrition-call.Got you on my roster now. I'm going to have you finish out this week's workouts as written. After our call, and after I see how you move this week, I'll write your workouts for next week.Thank you, sir!
	[Beau Baggett]: Sounds good. I’m completing these on Wednesday and Friday because I’ve got an overnight hike scheduled for the weekend. Talk to you on Friday.
	[Beau Baggett]: Camping trip? Sounds awesome
	[Beau Baggett]: Yeah backpack camping at Land Between the Lakes national recreation area. Got a group of guys from work and old college friends that need more voluntary hardship in their lives.
	[Beau Baggett]: This is the third time my back has flared up since 2020. It tends to be a gradual build up each time that I notice during squats and deadlifts. I saw a chiropractor in 2020. X-rays showed the lumbar discs in good shape. Chiropractor thought it was piriformis related. In 2021 a similar thing happened. Gradual build up of discomfort. On a volume deadlift day, felt a pop in the low back area. Super sore the rest of the day, applied heat and ice. Woke up the next day virtually pain free and was back to lifting heavy within 5 days. This time has been similar to the previous times except nothing has popped yet and the pain has been greater. My non-medical opinion is that the piriformis is irritating the sciatic nerve. Squats with an empty bar felt fine (2x5), moved to 135 and the pain was present but not terrible. 185x2 wasn’t any worse than 135 had been. 225 the pain was intense and I racked it after 2 reps. Dropped back to 135 but with the pain already flared up, the back off 135 hurt as much as 225.
	[Beau Baggett]: Hey Beau,Thanks for the detailed description. Hopefully whatever's bothering you will calm down soon and you can get back to regular training. Learning how to work around injuries is a skill that any lifetime lifter has to learn.The "pop" heard in your back is rarely injured tissue exploding. It's most often attributed to your facet joints. Facet joints are the interlocking joints of your vertebrae that keep your spine together. They pop just like your fingers do. And should be just as alarming as popping your knuckles. Likely you'll be an Upper Body Specialist until you can train pain free.Thanks, Beau.-Cameron

	[Beau Baggett]: Extra early session and short on time this morning, but got it in.
	[Beau Baggett]: The Texas Deadlift Bar knurling is more aggressive than the Rogue Ohio Power Bar (which is my other bar). I do usually hook grip the heavy work sets. I try to double overhand all the warmups and some of the volume/back off sets.
	[Beau Baggett]: Deadlift bars are nice for sure. There's one here that matches that description. Make sure you're hooking as much if not more than you are double overhand. You don't want to lose the feel of the hook grip. I did double overhand for all warm ups and mixed for heavies. Went in the gym one day and the mixed grip felt really weird. Had gotten more used to the double overhand. Decided to only use my mixed grip after that.

	[Beau Baggett]: I should have hook gripped this work set, but was using the Texas Deadlift Bar with a smaller diameter and thought I could double overhand 3

Workout Date: 2023-06-23

	[Beau Baggett]: The Slow Carb diet came from Tim Ferris’s book The 4 Hour Body. Really simple, just 5 rules: 1. No “white” carbohydrates 2. Repeat same few meals (meat, beans, veggies)3. Don’t drink calories 4. No fruit 5. 1 day off per week to eat anything It ends up being a meat/beans/veggie heavy diet which helps with our fiber targets. The beans and veggies seem to provide enough carbs for training. Nothing crazy, just wouldn’t mind dropping some body fat going into summer and elk hunting season in September.
	[Beau Baggett]: Hey Beau,Thanks for the detailed response.It seems like a reasonable approach to reduce calories. Let's give it a try and see how it goes. What can I program on my end to be helpful?
	[Beau Baggett]: Let’s keep it as-is for now with a Wednesday visual food diary and Friday subjective & objective check-in. Is body fat % a metric we can track through the app? I realize my bioimpedance scale may not be precisely accurate but I’m consistent with my timing and hydration status for weigh-ins so it should provide a decent measure (directionally, at least) for body fat %
	[Beau Baggett]: Sure thing. I just added bodyfat percentage on there.

Workout Date: 2023-08-02

	[Beau Baggett]: Agreed on grilled venison, mostly just the back straps, but I’ll occasionally get some good steak cut from the hams. A secret I’ve found more recently is using a Jaccard Blade Meat Tenderizer. I use it even on backstrap steaks (which are already tender but this makes then ridiculously tender). I save the shoulders for slow cooks in the oven or on the grill slathered with lard and seasoning and wrapped in foil. We grind the rest of our meat to use in making bratwursts or just freeze as ground meat for chili, spaghetti, burgers, etc.

	[Beau Baggett]: Using the Navy Method for measuring body fat % instead of the bioimpedance scale to try to remove hydration status from the variability.
	[Beau Baggett]: No, I don’t think I shed that much body fat in a week, but from my reading it seems the Navy Method calculation more closely aligns with DEXA results when comparing against other methods of measuring. Workout Date: 2024-12-06

	[Beau Baggett]: 😂 I’d say you know me pretty well Cam. “Nothing to report” usually = lack of motivation or some minor discouragement. I generally reserve my (sometimes) long-winded comments about how I’m feeling for our video calls, but since you asked I’ll give you a rundown that might help frame our conversation at the end of this month. Here’s where I’m at right now: Strength progress seems good. I’ve been happy with the weights I’ve been able to move on max effort days. Westside programming is actually a good fit for my personality. I like waking up early when it’s still dark, jumping in the ice bath, then going max effort on something for 1 rep 🔥 Nutritionally, I’m at the crossroads everyone faces because “we can’t have our leanness and eat cake too”. My lifetime strength goals have been to pull 500, squat 405, and bench 315 (arbitrary numbers I know, but milestone arbitrary numbers). But, I’ve pushed my body weight up close to 200lbs as recently as last year and was nowhere near hitting those lifts. And the reality is, I may never reach them. So, I have these competing interests of 1.) Pushing my body weight up with a goal of increased strength numbers even though they’ll likely be short of my goals, or 2.) Be muscular and lean and also not as strong (more aesthetically and athletically focused). It’s a choice I’m really torn about. It’s also an area where I’m more susceptible to be influenced than in other areas of my life. I can read one article by Carl Raghavan on startingstrength.com and I’m set on being purely strength focused, increased body weight (and body fat) be damned. Then I can remember the photo of beast NFL D-lineman Nick Bosa and realize that’s the type of physique I want to chase as my overarching goal. I know I’m not the only guy that wrestles with competing goals, and I apologize for the long message in response to your simple question, but you’ve been successfully serving as my navigator through this strength and nutrition world for 2 years now and I have a great deal of respect for your opinions about these things, and I know that the more clarity with which we can define the goal, the easier it will be for you to help design a training and nutrition framework to achieve it.
	[Beau Baggett]: Also, sorry for sending this picture of a shirtless dude, but I wanted you to have a reference point for my mention of Nick Bosa and the type of physique I would be pursuing. Not bodybuilding jacked or lean, but more jacked and lean than most. ﻿IMG_0338.jpeg 1.22 MB﻿
	[Beau Baggett]: Hey Beau,Thanks for the detailed response.I think there's a few parts to best answer this conundrum:1) Do what makes you happy - If you like being lean and having visible muscles, do that!2) Do what makes sense for your current season of life - If it's Thanksgiving, Christmas, and travel time, adjust your goals to how you'd like to maintain your diet during that time frame.3) Be realistic about your outcomes - The Good Lord has surely blessed you in many ways. The ability to look and perform like 21 year old NFL player is probably not one of those ways. I know you know this. But it's worth reiterating. Think these over. Let me know what you come up with.-Cam
	[Beau Baggett]: Wise advice as always, Cam. I’ll think it over and have something decided by our end of December call.
	[Beau Baggett]: Hey Beau. Glad you found it helpful. Reschedule that call to an earlier date if you'd like to talk it out little more while it's still fresh in our minds. Gave you a bit of follow-up feedback on your workout review too.
